22. HE natural Liberty of Man
1s to be free from any Supert-
our Power on Earth, and not to be under

m the Commonwealth; nor under the

ny Law, but what that Legillative ſhall
enaft, according to the Truit pur in ir.
Freedom then is not what Sr. R, F. tells

what he liſts, to live as he fre 4 and not
to be tyed by any Laws: but Freedom of
Men, under Government, 1s, to have a
ſtanding Rule to live by, common to eve-
ry one of that Society, and made by the
Legiſlative Power erected init. A Liber-
ty to follow my own Will ia all things,
where that Rule preſcribes not; not to
be ſubjeft to the inconitant, uncertain,
unknown, Arbitrary Will of another Man.
As Freedom of Nature rs to be under no
other reſtraint but the Law of Nature.
